	% -----
	\subsubsection{John 1} % *JOHN-1 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-1}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:1} % *JOHN-1-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-1}

				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					For 1--14 here, compare \hyperref[DC88-5]{DC 88:5--13} and some verses beyond that.
					}%
			IN the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					There are other definitions of \gr{πρὸς} in the accusative which could be relevant here, and connect to restoration scripture in different ways. Meaning 3c in BDAG is ``of goal, \textit{(aiming) at} or \textit{(striving) toward},'' which makes me think of Christ progressing and receiving a fulness, as in \hyperref[DC93]{DC 93}; meaning 3d is ``of relationship (hostile or friendly), \textit{against, for},'' making me think of how Christ was in favor of the Father's way of doing things in the plan of salvation; and meaning 3e is ``to indicate a connection by marking a point of reference,'' \textit{with reference/regard to},'' making me think of how Christ is the fulness of God manifest in a human body, and through him we are able to return to the Father. Meaning 3g is ``\textit{by, at, near},'' and that's the one that lists these verses in John 1.
					}
			God, and the Word was God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν ὁ} \hyperref[LOGOS]{\grl{λόγος}}\gr{, καὶ ὁ} \hyperref[LOGOS]{\grl{λόγος}} \gr{ἦν πρὸς τὸν θεόν, καὶ θεὸς ἦν ὁ} \hyperref[LOGOS]{\grl{λόγος}}\gr{.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		\paragraph{John 1:2} % *JOHN-1-2 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-2}

			The same was in the beginning with God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὗτος ἦν ἐν ἀρχῇ πρὸς τὸν θεόν.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:3} % *JOHN-1-3 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-3}

			All things were made by him; and without him was not any thing made that%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Note that the Greek from which we get the KJV puts \gr{ὃ γέγονεν} with the end of verse 3 instead of the beginning of verse 4. So you get verse 3 ending ``and without him was not any thing made that was made,'' and a period after that, and the entirety of verse 4 is ``In him was life; and the life was the light of men.'' But \gr{ὃ γέγονεν} at the end of verse 3 should get added to the beginning of 4, so the whole thing together should be, ``That which has come to be in him was life.'' I like that as a general statement of the transformative power of the new birth in Christ---whatever ``comes to be in him'' or is reborn through the power of the atonement just ``is life,'' is a force of living and progressing activity.
					}
			was made.%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					I read this verse in the light of \hyperref[DC88-5]{DC 88:5--13}, as showing the centrality of Christ to all existence. Taking a \textit{lot} of liberty with this verse and the previous one, I like to read it like this for a more accurate translation: ``That which has come to be in him was life, and that life was the light of men,'' and like this for one of the meanings: ``Whatever comes to be in him was a living force and that living force was the light of men,'' or even ``Whatever happens in him was a force of living activity, and that force of living activity was the light of men.'' The point is that the new birth in Christ creates a new force of living activity in us, the fountain of youth, springing up with energy and power for action in this life and eternal life in the world to come. See \hyperref[DC88-11]{DC 88:11--13}.
					}

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πάντα δι᾽ αὐτοῦ ἐγένετο, καὶ χωρὶς αὐτοῦ ἐγένετο οὐδὲ ἕν. ὃ γέγονεν}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]

					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:14} % *JOHN-1-14 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-14}

			And the Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					See Augustine, DDC I:26: ``And what was the manner of his coming, if not this: `The word was made flesh and lived among us.'? When we speak, the word which we hold in our mind becomes a sound in order that what we have in our mind may pass through ears of flesh into the listener's mind: this is called speech. Our thought, however, is not converted into the same sound, but remains intact in its own home, suffering no diminution from its change as it takes on the form of a word in order to make its way into the ears. In the same way the word of God suffered no change although it became flesh in order to live in us.''
					}
			(and we beheld his glory, the glory as of the only begotten of the Father,) full of grace and truth.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Καὶ ὁ} \hyperref[LOGOS]{\grl{λόγος}} \gr{σὰρξ ἐγένετο καὶ ἐσκήνωσεν ἐν ἡμῖν, καὶ ἐθεασάμεθα τὴν δόξαν αὐτοῦ, δόξαν ὡς μονογενοῦς παρὰ πατρός, πλήρης χάριτος καὶ ἀληθείας·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:15} % *JOHN-1-15 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-15}

			John bare witness of him, and cried, saying, This was he of whom I spake, He that cometh after me is preferred before me: for he was before%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					``Prior to me,'' but I wonder if it refers to a hierarchy of progression.
					}
			me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{(Ἰωάννης μαρτυρεῖ περὶ αὐτοῦ καὶ κέκραγεν λέγων· Οὗτος ἦν ὃν εἶπον· Ὁ ὀπίσω μου ἐρχόμενος ἔμπροσθέν μου γέγονεν, ὅτι πρῶτός μου ἦν·)}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					\item[ASV] John beareth witness of him, and crieth, saying, This was he of whom I said, He that cometh after me is become before me: for he was before me.
					\item[ESV] (John bore witness about him, and cried out, ``This was he of whom I said, `He who comes after me ranks before me, because he was before me.''')
					\item[NASB] John testified about Him and cried out, saying, ``This was He of whom I said, `He who comes after me has a higher rank than I, for He existed before me.'''
					\item[NIV] (John testified concerning him. He cried out, saying, ``This is the one I spoke about when I said, `He who comes after me has surpassed me because he was before me.''')
					\item[NRSV] (John testified to him and cried out, ``This was he of whom I said, `He who comes after me ranks ahead of me because he was before me.''')
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:18} % *JOHN-1-18 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-18}

			No man hath seen God at any time;%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					See \hyperref[DC67-11]{DC 67:11} and notes there.
					} 
			the only begotten Son, which is in the bosom%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					See the \hyperref[KOLPOS-TDNT]{TDNT} for \gr{κόλπος}; the meaning seems to come from a motif in which the \gr{κόλπος} represents the place of honor at a meal, occupied by someone special to the host.
					}
			of the Father, he hath declared him.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{θεὸν οὐδεὶς ἑώρακεν πώποτε· μονογενὴς θεὸς ὁ ὢν εἰς τὸν} \hyperref[KOLPOS]{\grl{κόλπον}} \gr{τοῦ πατρὸς ἐκεῖνος ἐξηγήσατο.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					\item[AB 4] No one has ever seen God; it is God the only Son, ever at the Father's side,\footnotemark who has revealed Him.
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}
				\footnotetext{%
					% \label{}%
					Translating \gr{κόλπος} as ``side.'' AB 17 discusses this passage but doesn't say much about \gr{κόλπος}, other than that ```bosom' connotes affection.'' 
					}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:19} % *JOHN-1-19 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-19}

			And this is the record of John, when the Jews sent priests and Levites from Jerusalem to ask him, Who art thou?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}

		\paragraph{John 1:33} % *JOHN-1-33 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-33}

			And I knew him not: but he that sent me to baptize with water, the same said unto me, Upon whom thou shalt see the Spirit descending, and remaining on him, the same is he which baptizeth with the Holy Ghost.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{κἀγὼ οὐκ ᾔδειν αὐτόν, ἀλλ᾽ ὁ πέμψας με βαπτίζειν ἐν ὕδατι ἐκεῖνός μοι εἶπεν· Ἐφ᾽ ὃν ἂν ἴδῃς τὸ πνεῦμα καταβαῖνον καὶ μένον ἐπ᾽ αὐτόν, οὗτός ἐστιν ὁ βαπτίζων ἐν πνεύματι ἁγίῳ·}

					% \item[BSV]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 1:34} % *JOHN-1-34 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-1-34}

			And I saw, and bare record that this is the Son of God.%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Note the Greek, which is different. My Nestle-Aland \textit{Novum Testamentum Graece} gives a different reading: after \gr{ἐστιν} it has \gr{ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ θεοῦ}. The \gr{ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ θεοῦ} reading receives a ``B'' level of certainty in the other Greek NT I have, indicating ``that the text is almost certain.'' So there is some debate here. MTCGNT 200: ``Instead of `the Son of God' several witnesses, cheifly Western (codex Bezae is defective here), read `the chosen one of God'...and a few read `the chosen Son of God'...On the basis of age and diversity of witnesses a majority of the Committee preferred the reading \gr{ὁ υἱὸς}, which is also in harmony with the theological terminology of the Fourth Evangelist.''
					}

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{κἀγὼ ἑώρακα, καὶ μεμαρτύρηκα ὅτι οὗτός ἐστιν ὁ ἐκλεκτὸς τοῦ θεοῦ.}

		% ---

			Then Jesus turned, and saw them following, and saith unto them, What seek ye? They said unto him, Rabbi, (which is to say, being interpreted, Master,) where dwellest thou?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{στραφεὶς δὲ ὁ Ἰησοῦς καὶ θεασάμενος αὐτοὺς ἀκολουθοῦντας λέγει αὐτοῖς· Τί ζητεῖτε; οἱ δὲ εἶπαν αὐτῷ· Ῥαββί (ὃ λέγεται μεθερμηνευόμενον Διδάσκαλε), ποῦ μένεις;}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει αὐτοῖς· Ἔρχεσθε καὶ ὄψεσθε. ἦλθαν οὖν καὶ εἶδαν ποῦ μένει, καὶ παρ᾽ αὐτῷ ἔμειναν τὴν ἡμέραν ἐκείνην· ὥρα ἦν ὡς δεκάτη.}

			One of the two which heard John speak, and followed him, was Andrew, Simon Peter's brother.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἦν Ἀνδρέας ὁ ἀδελφὸς Σίμωνος Πέτρου εἷς ἐκ τῶν δύο τῶν ἀκουσάντων παρὰ Ἰωάννου καὶ ἀκολουθησάντων αὐτῷ·}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

	\subsubsection{John 2} % *JOHN-2 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-2}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 2:1} % *JOHN-2-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-1}

			AND the third day%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					IVPBBC NT 253b: ```Third day' here does not refer to the third day of the week, because virgins were married on the fourth day (Wednesday) and widows on the fifth; nor does it seem to fit the count of days in 1:29, 35, 43. Presumably it simply means (as it normally did) `the third day after the event just narrated' (counting the days inclusively, so that it was the day after the next day). But ancient writers often bracketed off segments of their work by starting and ending on the same note (a practice called \textit{inclusio}); thus John may use this designation to point toward \hyperref[JOHN-2-19]{2:19} and link this story (2:1-11) with the prediction of Jesus' death and resurrection.''
					
					ZIBBC NT 02 71: ```The third day' is to be counted from the last event narrated, Jesus' encounter with Nathanael. Including the first day in one's calculation, this means two days later. In conjunction with John's initial testimony to Jesus in 1:19-28 and the three references to `the next day' in 1:29, 35, and 43, this completes an entire week of activity.
						\begin{compactdesc}
							\item[Day 1:] John the Baptist testifies concerning Jesus (1:19-28).
							\item[Day 2:] John the Baptist's encounter with Jesus (1:29-34; `the next day').
							\item[Day 3:] John the Baptist refers two of his disciples to Jesus (1:35-39; `the next day').
							\item[Day 4:] Andrew introduces his brother Peter to Jesus (1:40-42).
							\item[Day 5:] Philip and Nathanael follow Jesus (1:43-51; `the next day').
							\item[Day 6:] No information is given (Sabbath?).
							\item[Day 7:] Jesus attends the wedding at Cana (2:1-11; `on the third day').''
						\end{compactdesc}
					PNTC suggests that the day of the wine miracle was the Sabbath and draws a parallel with the week of the creation.
					}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 2:4} % *JOHN-2-4 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-4}

			Jesus saith unto her, Woman,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					BDAG 184b, in the entry for \gr{γυνή}, says: ``The vocative (\gr{ὦ}) \gr{γύναι} is by no means a disrespectful form of address.'' NIDNTT III:1055 says that ``\gr{γυνή} in the vocative is used as an address with no irreverent secondary meaning (Homer, Sophocles and others),'' but that is for older Greek. Speaking of the NT in particular, we read that ``\gr{γυνή} in the vocative is common as an address'' (III:1058). LSJ 363b (PDF 434) does say, in the entry for \gr{γυνή}, ``vocative, as a term of respect or affection, \textit{mistress, lady}.'' I seem to remember Elder Holland (?) once mentioning this story and how Christ spoke to his mother in a very respectful way. I'm not sure how well that use in the LSJ applies here, and in the NT sources I have seen, there isn't any mention of the form of address as something particularly respectful, though they clearly say that it isn't \textit{disrespectful} either. It seems that \gr{γυνή} in the vocative is just more of a common way of addressing a woman, neither uniquely respectful or disrespectful; it would be kind of like saying ``hey girl'' in a situation where that would be appropriate, or maybe ``ma'am,'' depending on their age and your relationship to them. It's just a way of addressing a woman. That's how I'll think about it for now (1/5/23), until I read some other NT-specific sources that say otherwise.
					}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 2:6} % *JOHN-2-6 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-6}

			And there were set there six waterpots of stone, after the manner of the purifying of the Jews, containing two or three firkins apiece.%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Here are the key words in this passage:
						\begin{compactdesc}
							\item[\gr{λίθιναι}] ``(made of) stone'' (BDAG 527b).
							\item[\gr{ὑδρίαι}] ``water jar'' (BDAG 910a).
							\item[\gr{χωροῦσαι}] ``to have room for, \textit{hold, contain}'' (BDAG 974b).
							\item[\gr{ἀνὰ}] ``each, apiece'' (BDAG 50b).
							\item[\gr{μετρητὰς}] ``a liquid measure of about 40 liters'' (BDAG 570b).
						\end{compactdesc}
					Putting them all together, the text says that there were six stone water jars, each full of two or three measures of water, meaning they were each holding between 80 and 120 liters of water. Suppose they were all the way full at 120 liters. That's 120,000 cubic centimeters---to visualize, take a box that is one square meter in size and raise it twelve centimeters, then fill the entire thing with liquid. That's how much each one holds, and you need six of those, so it's quite a bit. For \gr{κατὰ τὸν καθαρισμὸν τῶν Ἰουδαίων κείμεναι}, see TDNTA 337--339, especially the discussions of ``cultic cleansing.'' The important thing is that the vessels themselves needed to be cleansed: ``Restoration of cleanness is primarily by water (washing, sprinkling, or bathing), though sin offering may also be required. Vessels, too, are cleansed by water (dipping or scalding); sometimes they may have to be destroyed'' (338). So these were vessels which were being cleansed or had been cleansed according to this tradition. Based on the beginning of verse 7, it appears as though the jars were empty at first, and Christ had people fill them, which would have been quite a chore, actually---you'd have to either get the water from some vessel even larger, or from a well or other source, or carry the stone jars (which must have been heavy) to a water source and fill them, after which they would have been even heavier. It's not like they could just turn on the hose here---he was asking them to do some serious work, it seems.
					}

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἦσαν δὲ ἐκεῖ λίθιναι ὑδρίαι ἓξ κατὰ τὸν καθαρισμὸν τῶν Ἰουδαίων κείμεναι, χωροῦσαι ἀνὰ μετρητὰς δύο ἢ τρεῖς.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

		\paragraph{John 2:7} % *JOHN-2-7 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-7}

			Jesus saith unto them, Fill the waterpots with water. And they filled them up to the brim.

			\begin{framed}

				\begin{compactdesc}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 2:8} % *JOHN-2-8 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-8}

			And he saith unto them, Draw out now, and bear unto the governor%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{ἀρχιτρίκλινος} appears three times in the NT---once in this verse, and twice in the next verse, so it's only in this story. The word is at BDAG 122b: ``(\gr{τρίκλινος} a banquet room `with three couches') (the slave who was responsible for managing a banquet...\textit{head waiter, butler}.'' From NIDNTT III:922: ```The point of the story does not lie in the transformation of water into wine as such, but in the comment of the steward, who does not even know what has happened' (Lindars, op. cit., 131; cf. v. 9). Lindars concludes: `If the form-critical analysis...is accepted, the transformation is regarded as unhistorical, so that the miracle requires no explanation. But if the narrative is accepted as substantially historical, it is essential to accept the miraculous element along with it, and to assume that Jesus could and did perform the miracle as it is described' (ibid.). The story has been seen not only as symbolic of Jesus' transforming power, but also as a symbolic anticipation of the new age foretold in the OT and deutero-canonical writings in which wine would be present in abundance.''
					}
			of the feast. And they bare it.

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 2:10} % *JOHN-2-10 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-2-10}

			And saith unto him, Every man at the beginning doth set forth good wine; and when men have well drunk, then that which is worse: but thou hast kept the good wine%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					What strikes me about this story is Christ's transformation of a part of a Jewish cleansing ritual into something of value and great abundance. Based on \gr{κατὰ τὸν καθαρισμὸν τῶν Ἰουδαίων κείμεναι} in verse 6, the jars were being or had been purified, a process which probably would have been carried out with water. Christ had them fill the jars with water, perhaps partially in imitation of this cleansing ceremony. But then he took this old ritual and exercised his power and created something of value, the wine, in great abundance, more than enough for everyone. So this is not only a demonstration of his abundant power which is more than enough for the needs of everyone, but also an indication of how he was turning the old ways into something new---he was transforming the old rituals and making of them something new, something better.
					}
			until now.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὗτος ἦλθεν πρὸς αὐτὸν νυκτὸς καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ῥαββί, οἴδαμεν ὅτι ἀπὸ θεοῦ ἐλήλυθας διδάσκαλος· οὐδεὶς γὰρ δύναται ταῦτα τὰ σημεῖα ποιεῖν ἃ σὺ ποιεῖς, ἐὰν μὴ ᾖ ὁ θεὸς μετ᾽ αὐτοῦ.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 3:8} % *JOHN-3-8 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-3-8}

			The wind%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The word is \gr{πνεῦμα} here, so this verse could just as easily be seen as talking about the Spirit. ABET has a footnote saying, ``The word for `wind' is the same as that for `spirit' (or `Spirit').''
					}
			bloweth%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{LCHRIST-036-pnei}%
					The word is \gr{πνέω}. From BDAG 837b, PDF 462:
						\begin{compactitem}
							\item[1] \textbf{to move as wind with relatively rapid motion, \textit{blow}}
							\item[2] \textbf{to emit an odor, \textit{breathe out}}
							\item[3] \textbf{with accusative, \textit{breathe something out}}
						\end{compactitem}
					John 3:8 is listed under definition \#3. Notice that it says \gr{τὸ πνεῦμα πνεῖ}, and there are clear etymological similarities between \gr{πνεῦμα} and \gr{πνέω}; it's kind of like a cognate accusative, except a cognate nominative. \gr{πνέω} appears in Beekes's 2010 etymological dictionary of Greek, on 1213 (PDF 632). \gr{πνέω}, meaning ``to blow, breathe, respire, smell,'' comes possibly from an Indoeuropean root \textit{pneu-} meaning ``breathe, cough, smell.'' You then get the derivative \gr{πνεῦμα}, meaning ``wind, breeze, breath, ghost.'' The fundamental sense of the verb is basically air moving around, and the \gr{πνεῦμα} is the neuter verb that comes from the noun, whose meaning could be described (at least from an etymological perspective) as ``the air which is moved around'' when something is engaged in an act of \gr{πνέω}. See also \hyperref[PNEUMA]{\grl{πνεῦμα}}, where definition \#1 is ``air in movement, \textit{blowing, breathing}.''
					}
			where it \hyperref[LIST]{listeth}, and thou hearest the sound thereof, but canst not tell whence it cometh, and whither it goeth:
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					I connect what is said here, about those who are born of the Spirit, to the instruction given in \hyperref[DC84-44]{DC 84:44}.
					}%
			so%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{LCHRIST-036-outos}%
					I am not totally sure what inference Christ is drawing with the \gr{οὕτως} here, or what comparison he is trying to make. This seems like a bit of a stretch, but maybe he is saying that a person who has been born from the spirit is in the same situation as Nicodemos, of not knowing where the Spirit is coming from or what it is going on its promptings; that person experiences the Spirit and revelation from it but doesn't always know the causes or the intentions or the results. I'm not sure. 
					}
			is every one that is born of the Spirit.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τὸ} \hyperref[PNEUMA]{\grl{πνεῦμα}} \gr{ὅπου θέλει πνεῖ, καὶ τὴν φωνὴν αὐτοῦ ἀκούεις, ἀλλ᾽ οὐκ οἶδας πόθεν ἔρχεται καὶ ποῦ ὑπάγει· οὕτως ἐστὶν πᾶς ὁ γεγεννημένος ἐκ τοῦ πνεύματος.}
					% \item[SNT]
					\item[BSV] Spiritus ubi vult spirat et vocem eius audis sed non scis unde veniat et quo vadat sic est omnis qui natus est ex Spiritu
					\item[ASV] The wind bloweth where it will, and thou hearest the voice thereof, but knowest not whence it cometh, and whither it goeth: so is every one that is born of the Spirit.
					\item[ESV] The wind blows where it wishes, and you hear its sound, but you do not know where it comes from or where it goes. So it is with everyone who is born of the Spirit.''
					\item[NASB] ``The wind blows where it wishes and you hear the sound of it, but do not know where it comes from and where it is going; so is everyone who is born of the Spirit.''
					\item[NIV] The wind blows wherever it pleases. You hear its sound, but you cannot tell where it comes from or where it is going. So it is with everyone born of the Spirit.''
					\item[NRSV] The wind blows where it chooses, and you hear the sound of it, but you do not know where it comes from or where it goes. So it is with everyone who is born of the Spirit.''
					% \item[AB ]
					\item[SPNT] The wind blows where it pleases and you hear its sound, but you do not know where it comes from or where it is going. So it is with anyone born of the Spirit.''
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 3:16} % *JOHN-3-16 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-3-16}

			For God so loved the world, that he gave his only begotten%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{LCHRIST-036-monogene}%
					For \gr{μονογενής}, from BDAG 658a (PDF 370), meaning \#2: ``pertaining to being the only one of its kind or class, \textit{unique (in kind)} of something that is the only example of its category.'' On this meaning you could just translate it into English as ``only'' or as my reader's lexicon says, ``unique (in kind),'' just following BDAG. Later in the discussion of meaning \#2, there is a discussion of the ``begotten'' meaning that we still find in the KJV, which meaning would be ``(according to his real being: i.e. uniquely divine as God's son and transcending all others alleged to be gods) or a \textit{uniquely begotten deity}...some...prefer to regard \gr{μονογενής} as somewhat heightened in meaning in John and 1 John to \textit{only-begotten} or \textit{begotten of the Only One}, in view of the emphasis on [the fact that Christ \gr{ἐκ θεοῦ ἐγεννήθησαν}] (John 1:13 and elsewhere); in this case it would be analogous to \gr{πρωτότοκος} (Romans 8:29, Colossians 1:15, al.).'' Most translations into English have ``only'' or something like ``one and only''; a few, some older, have ``only begotten.''
					}
			Son, that whosoever believeth in him should not perish, but have everlasting life.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Οὕτως γὰρ ἠγάπησεν ὁ θεὸς τὸν} \hyperref[KOSMOS]{\grl{κόσμον}} \gr{ὥστε τὸν υἱὸν τὸν μονογενῆ ἔδωκεν, ἵνα πᾶς ὁ πιστεύων εἰς αὐτὸν μὴ ἀπόληται ἀλλὰ ἔχῃ ζωὴν αἰώνιον.}

			\end{framed}

			\label{JOHN-3-20}

			For every one that doeth evil%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Note that the word here is \hyperref[PHAULOS]{\grl{φαῦλα}}, which is different from the ending of the previous verse, where it uses the adjective \hyperref[PONEROS]{\grl{πονηρὰ}}. On this change in word, see NICNT page 234 note 87, and also page 217 note 30. In that second note, the author explains that it's part of John's style to repeat a statement with minor word variations but with no apparent difference in actual meaning. There are a number of examples given in the note, and I think the author sees this difference as another example. EBCA NT PDF 647: ``The difference between believers and unbelievers does not lie in the guilt or innocence of either; it lies in the different attitudes they take toward the `light.' Unbelievers shrink from the light because it exposes their sin; believers willingly come to the light so that their real motives may be revealed (see also \hyperref[1JOHN-1-8]{1Jn 1:8--9}). Furthermore, darkness (defined as love of evil deeds) keeps people from responding to the light (\hyperref[1JOHN-1-5]{1Jn 1:5}). All of us are held accountable for our actions, and the choice is ours: evil deeds or truth.''
					}
			hateth the light, neither cometh to the light, lest his deeds should be
				\footnote{%
					\label{ELENCHO}%
					``Reproved'' is not right here, I think. The word is \gr{ἐλέγχω}, BDAG 278b:
						\begin{compactdesc}
							\item[1] \textbf{to scrutinize or examine carefully, \textit{bring to light, expose, set forth}}
							\item[2] \textbf{to bring a person to the point of recognizing wrongdoing, \textit{convict, convince}} someone of something, \textit{point something out to someone}
							\item[3] \textbf{to express strong disapproval of someone's action, \textit{reprove, correct}}
							\item[4] \textbf{to penalize for wrongdoing, \textit{punish, discipline}}
						\end{compactdesc}
					So the ``reprove'' meaning is there, but in the context of this verse, with the idea of not coming to the ``light'' (and the ideas in verse 21 as well), I think the first meaning of \gr{ἐλέγχω} seems more suitable here.
					}%
			reproved.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πᾶς γὰρ ὁ} \hyperref[PHAULOS]{\grl{φαῦλα}} \gr{πράσσων μισεῖ τὸ φῶς καὶ οὐκ ἔρχεται πρὸς τὸ φῶς, ἵνα μὴ ἐλεγχθῇ τὰ ἔργα αὐτοῦ·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

			He must increase,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{αὐξάνω}. BDAG 132b meaning \#2.b: ``This is usu. considered a direct ref. to success in attracting followers, but \gr{αὐ.} can also be used of the increase of sunlight (Calendarium of Antiochus [c. 200 a.d.] on Dec. 25 \gr{Ἡλίου γενέθλιον· αὔξει φῶς} [FBoll, SBHeidAk 1910, 16; Abh. p. 40ff]; Cosmas of Jerusalem [FCumont, Natalis Invicti: Extr. des compt. rend. de l'Ac. des Inscr. et Bell. Lett. 1911, 292f]). Cp. 3:19--21, where \gr{φῶς} occurs five times, and marks the leading concept. May this not also be true of 3:30? At any rate the Gk. and Lat. fathers understood 3:30 in the solar sense.'' So think of \hyperref[DC50-24]{DC 50:24} and related scriptures, for example. I looked in ACCS for commentary on this verse in terms of ``light,'' as said in BDAG here, but there wasn't anything, so I'd have to look elsewhere if I wanted to find it. Note that \hyperref[LUKE-2-52]{Luke 2:52} is \gr{προέκοπτεν} and not a form of \gr{αὐξάνω}.
					}

			Jesus answered and said unto her, If thou knewest the gift of God, and who it is that saith to thee, Give me to drink; thou wouldest have asked of him, and he would have given thee 
				\footnote{%
					See Pook's ``\hyperref[TTW-LIFE-ENERGY-0001]{Fountain of Youth}.'' In my mind, one of the most important concepts in the scriptures. It's a force of energy and power inside you that enriches every aspect of your life, enables you to do things you wouldn't be able to do otherwise, and quickens you physically and mentally.
					
					See \hyperref[ZAO]{\grl{ζάω}} for ``living'' (``to live''), and \hyperref[HUDOR]{\grl{ὕδωρ}} for ``water''; the latter is the one you want to check for the special phrase ``living water.'' 
					}%
			living%

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 4:14} % *JOHN-4-14 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-4-14}

			But whosoever drinketh of the water that I shall give him shall never thirst; but the water that I shall give him shall be in him%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					I think the fact that this well is ``in us'' is significant. We draw from Christ's source, but the source then gets inside of us and becomes something that ourselves can draw from, and that others can too. It makes me think of the order of the priesthood and how we join to receive the priesthood through Christ, and then over time we will grow into becoming more like Christ, with the source inside of us.
					}
			a well of%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Compare \hyperref[DC63-23]{DC 63:23}.
					}
			water springing%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Participle of \gr{ἅλλομαι}; the word appears only three times in the NT. The other two are in \hyperref[ACTS-3-8]{Acts 3:8}, the second ``leaping'' in that verse in the KJV translation (first is \gr{ἐξάλλομαι}), and \hyperref[ACTS-14-10]{14:10}, ``leaped.'' BDAG 40b gives two definitions---the first is ``literally to make a quick leaping movement, \textit{leap, spring up},'' and the second is ``figurative, of the quick movement of inanimate things: to spring up from a source, of water \textit{well up, bubble up}.'' At CGL 62b, the first definition is ``\textbf{leap, jump, spring, bound},'' and the fifth is ``(of a spring of water) \textbf{well up} NT.'' So the ``well up'' definition is noted as belonging to the NT here. CGL also gives four other definitions, and all of them involve fast, almost sudden or jerky motions---an arrow ``leaping forward'' from a bowstring, an echo bouncing off of something, a mousetrap springing, and an eye twitching. The meaning of the word seems to involve a motion that is sudden or very different from previous motions, and that is moving upward or forward. From that I'm not sure that ``well up'' is the best English translation; the image it creates is too slow. ``Spring forth'' might be better, but if you want to go further, I think you get to the idea of a fountain or even a geyser just spewing the water out in a jet. When you look at the other uses of the word---both involving men who had significant physical problems that stopped them from living a normal life, who were then healed by God's power and began to experience life in an entirely new way, beginning with jumping into the air, an action they could not have ever done---then it seems clear that we need something stronger than just ``well up'' or even ``springing up'' maybe. The living waters become a \gr{πηγή}, a fountain, which produces water that does inside of us what those men do in Acts when they are healed: they jump for joy, they experience life in a new way. That is what the water is doing inside of us---when we get that force and energy inside of us, what will we do? How will we be different? What will we do that we didn't do before? What will we experience that we didn't experience before? What choices will we make about how we live that we didn't have the courage to make before?
					
					This is very closely related to the \hyperref[NEWBIRTH]{new birth} and to becoming like a child (see \hyperref[MOSIAH-3-19]{Mosiah 3:19}); see also \hyperref[DC84-33]{DC 84:33}, 
					}
			up into everlasting life.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὃς δ᾽ ἂν πίῃ ἐκ τοῦ ὕδατος οὗ ἐγὼ δώσω αὐτῷ, οὐ μὴ διψήσει εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, ἀλλὰ τὸ ὕδωρ ὃ δώσω αὐτῷ γενήσεται ἐν αὐτῷ πηγὴ ὕδατος} \hyperref[HALLOMAI]{\grl{ἁλλομένου}} \gr{εἰς ζωὴν αἰώνιον.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-4-22}

			Ye worship ye know not what: we know what we worship:%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The translation should be: ``You worship that which you do not know; we worship that which we do know.''
					}
			for salvation is of the Jews.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὑμεῖς προσκυνεῖτε ὃ οὐκ οἴδατε, ἡμεῖς προσκυνοῦμεν ὃ οἴδαμεν, ὅτι ἡ σωτηρία ἐκ τῶν Ἰουδαίων ἐστίν·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 4:23} % *JOHN-4-23 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-4-23}

			But the hour cometh, and now is, when the true worshippers shall worship the Father in spirit and in truth: for the Father seeketh such to worship him.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀλλὰ ἔρχεται ὥρα καὶ νῦν ἐστιν, ὅτε οἱ ἀληθινοὶ προσκυνηταὶ προσκυνήσουσιν τῷ πατρὶ ἐν πνεύματι καὶ ἀληθείᾳ, καὶ γὰρ ὁ πατὴρ τοιούτους ζητεῖ τοὺς προσκυνοῦντας αὐτόν·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 4:24} % *JOHN-4-24 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-4-24}

			God is a Spirit:%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					I take this to be not a metaphysical statement about the basic ontological category God belongs to, but that God has the property of being \gr{πνευματικός}, in the way that we will get that property later. See \hyperref[JOHN-3-5]{John 3:5--6} and \hyperref[1CORINTHIANS-2-14]{1 Corinthians 2:14--15}, including my Rainbows essay on that topic.
					}
			and they that worship him must worship him%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Compare BY, \hyperref[EMS-1865-BY-8-OCT-1865]{8 October 1865}: ``God is a spirit and seeks us to worship him in spirit and in truth when you and I see a resurrected being we will see a spirit nothing but the spirit.''
					} 
			in spirit and in truth.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πνεῦμα ὁ θεός, καὶ τοὺς προσκυνοῦντας αὐτὸν ἐν πνεύματι καὶ ἀληθείᾳ δεῖ προσκυνεῖν.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---

			The woman saith unto him, I know that Messias cometh, which is called Christ: when he is come, he will tell us all things.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει αὐτῷ ἡ γυνή· Οἶδα ὅτι Μεσσίας ἔρχεται, ὁ λεγόμενος χριστός· ὅταν ἔλθῃ ἐκεῖνος, ἀναγγελεῖ ἡμῖν ἅπαντα.}

		% ---

			And upon this came his disciples, and marvelled that he talked with the woman: yet no man said, What seekest thou? or, Why talkest thou with her?

					\item[SBLG] \gr{Καὶ ἐπὶ τούτῳ ἦλθαν οἱ μαθηταὶ αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἐθαύμαζον ὅτι μετὰ γυναικὸς ἐλάλει· οὐδεὶς μέντοι εἶπεν· Τί ζητεῖς; ἢ τί λαλεῖς μετ᾽ αὐτῆς;}

		% ---

			Jesus saith unto them, My meat%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{βρῶμά}; see \hyperref[1CORINTHIANS-3-2]{1 Corinthians 3:2} for the development or spiritual maturation process we need to undergo in order to come closer to the place that Christ is at in this respect.
					}
			is to do the will of him that sent me, and to finish his work.%

					% \label{}%
					See \hyperref[DC76-106]{DC 76:106}.
					}

		% ---

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-4-43}

			Now after two days he departed thence, and went into Galilee.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Μετὰ δὲ τὰς δύο ἡμέρας ἐξῆλθεν ἐκεῖθεν εἰς τὴν Γαλιλαίαν·}

			Then when he was come into Galilee, the Galilæans received him, having seen all the things that he did at Jerusalem at the feast: for they also went unto the feast.

	% -----
		\label{JOHN-5}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:1} % *JOHN-5-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-1}

			AFTER this there was a feast of the Jews; and Jesus went up to Jerusalem.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Μετὰ ταῦτα ἦν ἑορτὴ τῶν Ἰουδαίων, καὶ ἀνέβη Ἰησοῦς εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:2} % *JOHN-5-2 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-2}

			Now there is at Jerusalem by the sheep market a pool, which is called in the Hebrew tongue Bethesda,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					ZIBBC NT 02 120: ``Bethesda' may mean `house of (divine) mercy'---which would be a fitting term, given the desperate state of the people lying there in hope of miraculous healing---or `house of the two springs,' or the name may be derived from the root `pour out' or `slope.'''
					}
			having five porches.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔστιν δὲ ἐν τοῖς Ἱεροσολύμοις ἐπὶ τῇ προβατικῇ κολυμβήθρα ἡ ἐπιλεγομένη Ἑβραϊστὶ Βηθεσδά, πέντε στοὰς ἔχουσα·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:3} % *JOHN-5-3 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-3}

			In these lay a great multitude of impotent%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					It's actually a participle from \gr{ἀσθενέω} here, which is related to \gr{ἀσθένεια}, a general term for ``weakness.'' It makes me think of \hyperref[ALMA-7-11]{Alma 7:11}.
					}
			folk, of blind, halt, withered, waiting for the moving of the water.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐν ταύταις κατέκειτο πλῆθος τῶν ἀσθενούντων, τυφλῶν, χωλῶν, ξηρῶν.}
					% \item[SNT]

		% ---

			For an angel went down at a certain season into the pool, and troubled the water: whosoever then first after the troubling of the water stepped in was made whole of whatsoever disease he had.

			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἦν δέ τις ἄνθρωπος ἐκεῖ τριάκοντα ὀκτὼ ἔτη ἔχων ἐν τῇ ἀσθενείᾳ αὐτοῦ·}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{τοῦτον ἰδὼν ὁ Ἰησοῦς κατακείμενον, καὶ γνοὺς ὅτι πολὺν ἤδη χρόνον ἔχει, λέγει αὐτῷ· Θέλεις ὑγιὴς γενέσθαι;}

		\paragraph{John 5:6} % *JOHN-5-6 
		% ---

			When Jesus saw him lie, and knew that he had been now a long time in that case, he saith unto him, Wilt thou be made whole?

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀπεκρίθη αὐτῷ ὁ ἀσθενῶν· Κύριε, ἄνθρωπον οὐκ ἔχω ἵνα ὅταν ταραχθῇ τὸ ὕδωρ βάλῃ με εἰς τὴν κολυμβήθραν· ἐν ᾧ δὲ ἔρχομαι ἐγὼ ἄλλος πρὸ ἐμοῦ καταβαίνει.}

		% ---

			The Jews therefore said unto him that was cured, It is the sabbath day: it is not lawful for thee to carry thy bed.

			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔλεγον οὖν οἱ Ἰουδαῖοι τῷ τεθεραπευμένῳ· Σάββατόν ἐστιν, καὶ οὐκ ἔξεστίν σοι ἆραι τὸν κράβαττον.}

		\paragraph{John 5:11} % *JOHN-5-11

		% ---

			Afterward Jesus findeth him in the temple, and said unto him, Behold, thou art made whole: sin no more, lest a worse thing come unto thee.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μετὰ ταῦτα εὑρίσκει αὐτὸν ὁ Ἰησοῦς ἐν τῷ ἱερῷ καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἴδε ὑγιὴς γέγονας· μηκέτι ἁμάρτανε, ἵνα μὴ χεῖρόν σοί τι γένηται.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-18}

			Therefore the Jews sought the more to kill him, because he not only had broken the sabbath, but said also that God was his Father, making himself equal with God.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{διὰ τοῦτο οὖν μᾶλλον ἐζήτουν αὐτὸν οἱ Ἰουδαῖοι ἀποκτεῖναι ὅτι οὐ μόνον ἔλυε τὸ σάββατον, ἀλλὰ καὶ πατέρα ἴδιον ἔλεγε τὸν θεόν, ἴσον ἑαυτὸν ποιῶν τῷ θεῷ.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:19} % *JOHN-5-19 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-19}

			Then answered Jesus and said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, The Son can do nothing of himself, but what he seeth%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Compare our position with respect to Christ---see \hyperref[2NEPHI-31-12]{2 Nephi 31:12 and 17}.
					}
			the Father do: for what things soever he doeth, these also doeth the Son likewise.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀπεκρίνατο οὖν ὁ Ἰησοῦς καὶ ἔλεγεν αὐτοῖς· Ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν, οὐ δύναται ὁ υἱὸς ποιεῖν ἀφ᾽ ἑαυτοῦ οὐδὲν ἐὰν μή τι βλέπῃ τὸν πατέρα ποιοῦντα· ἃ γὰρ ἂν ἐκεῖνος ποιῇ, ταῦτα καὶ ὁ υἱὸς ὁμοίως ποιεῖ.}

					% \item[BSV]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:20} % *JOHN-5-20 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-20}

			For the Father loveth the Son, and sheweth him all things that himself doeth: and he will shew him greater works than these, that ye may marvel.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὁ γὰρ πατὴρ φιλεῖ τὸν υἱὸν καὶ πάντα δείκνυσιν αὐτῷ ἃ αὐτὸς ποιεῖ, καὶ μείζονα τούτων δείξει αὐτῷ ἔργα, ἵνα ὑμεῖς θαυμάζητε.}

		% ---

			For as the Father raiseth up the dead, and \hyperref[QUICKEN]{quickeneth} them; even so the Son \hyperref[QUICKEN]{quickeneth} whom he will.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὥσπερ γὰρ ὁ πατὴρ ἐγείρει τοὺς νεκροὺς καὶ} \hyperref[ZOOPOIEO]{\grl{ζῳοποιεῖ}}\gr{, οὕτως καὶ ὁ υἱὸς οὓς θέλει} \hyperref[ZOOPOIEO]{\grl{ζῳοποιεῖ}}\gr{.}

			For the Father judgeth no man, but hath committed all judgment unto the Son:

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐδὲ γὰρ ὁ πατὴρ κρίνει οὐδένα, ἀλλὰ τὴν κρίσιν πᾶσαν δέδωκεν τῷ υἱῷ,}

		\paragraph{John 5:23} % *JOHN-5-23 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-23}

			That all men should honour the Son, even as they honour the Father. He that honoureth not the Son honoureth not the Father which hath sent him.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἵνα πάντες τιμῶσι τὸν υἱὸν καθὼς τιμῶσι τὸν πατέρα. ὁ μὴ τιμῶν τὸν υἱὸν οὐ τιμᾷ τὸν πατέρα τὸν πέμψαντα αὐτόν.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:24} % *JOHN-5-24 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-24}

			Verily, verily, I say unto you, He that heareth my word, and believeth on him that sent me, hath everlasting life, and shall not come into condemnation; but is passed%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					A form of \gr{μεταβαίνω}; BDAG 565b, meaning \#2 gives ``\textbf{to change from one state or condition to another state, \textit{pass, pass on}}.'' See \hyperref[1JOHN-3-14]{1 John 3:14} and \hyperref[2NEPHI-9-15]{2 Nephi 9:15}.
					}
			from death unto life.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι ὁ τὸν λόγον μου ἀκούων καὶ πιστεύων τῷ πέμψαντί με ἔχει ζωὴν αἰώνιον, καὶ εἰς κρίσιν οὐκ ἔρχεται ἀλλὰ μεταβέβηκεν ἐκ τοῦ θανάτου εἰς τὴν ζωήν.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:25} % *JOHN-5-25 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-25}

			Verily, verily, I say unto you, The hour is coming, and now is, when the dead shall hear the voice of the Son of God: and they that hear shall live.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι ἔρχεται ὥρα καὶ νῦν ἐστιν ὅτε οἱ νεκροὶ ἀκούσουσιν τῆς φωνῆς τοῦ υἱοῦ τοῦ θεοῦ καὶ οἱ ἀκούσαντες ζήσουσιν.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:26} % *JOHN-5-26 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-26}

			For as the Father hath life in himself; so hath he given%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					The use of \gr{δίδωμι} here reminds me of \hyperref[MATTHEW-28-18]{Matthew 28:18}.
					}
			to the Son to have life in himself;%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					There must be many ways to interpret this verse, but I take it as a statement about how Christ is the kind of being through whom others can receive the fulness of the priesthood; see \hyperref[DC107-3]{DC 107:3}, but also places like \hyperref[JOHN-10-18]{John 10:18}, where it's more explicit. We can become Christ's children (\hyperref[MOSIAH-5-7]{Mosiah 5:7}) and through him receive life because he has the power of live in himself in the way that Heavenly Father (and Heavenly Mother) must. This is what it means that the priesthood we receive and have to do with is ``the holy priesthood, after the order of the Son of God,'' rather than ``after the order of God.'' We come unto God through Christ---see \hyperref[JOHN-14-6]{John 14:6}, ``no man cometh unto the Father, but by me.''
					}
			
			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὥσπερ γὰρ ὁ πατὴρ ἔχει ζωὴν ἐν ἑαυτῷ, οὕτως καὶ τῷ υἱῷ ἔδωκεν ζωὴν ἔχειν ἐν ἑαυτῷ·}
					\item[AB 129] Indeed, just as the Father possesses life in Himself,
									so has He granted that the Son also possess life in himself.
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:27} % *JOHN-5-27 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-27}

			And hath given him authority to execute judgment also, because he is the Son of man.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ ἐξουσίαν ἔδωκεν αὐτῷ κρίσιν ποιεῖν, ὅτι υἱὸς ἀνθρώπου ἐστίν.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:28} % *JOHN-5-28 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-28}

			Marvel not at this: for the hour is coming, in the which all that are in the graves shall hear his voice,

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μὴ θαυμάζετε τοῦτο, ὅτι ἔρχεται ὥρα ἐν ᾗ πάντες οἱ ἐν τοῖς μνημείοις ἀκούσουσιν τῆς φωνῆς αὐτοῦ}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]

					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:29} % *JOHN-5-29 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-29}

				\footnote{%
					\label{JOHN-5-29-76}%
					This is the key verse which JS and Sidney Rigdon read in February 1832, that led to section 76 of the DC. See \hyperref[DC76]{that section} for discussion.
					}%
			And shall come forth; they that have done good, unto the resurrection of life; and they that have done evil, unto the resurrection of damnation.%
				\footnote{%
					\label{JOHN-5-29-NOTE-damnation}%
					\gr{εἰς ἀνάστασιν κρίσεως}. See \hyperref[DAMNATION-new-testament]{this study} at \hyperref[DAMNATION]{Damnation} for analysis.
					}

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ ἐκπορεύσονται οἱ τὰ ἀγαθὰ ποιήσαντες εἰς ἀνάστασιν ζωῆς, οἱ δὲ τὰ φαῦλα πράξαντες εἰς ἀνάστασιν} \hyperref[KRISIS]{\grl{κρίσεως}}\gr{.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:30} % *JOHN-5-30 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-30}

			I can of mine own self do nothing: as I hear, I judge: and my judgment is just; because I seek not mine own will, but the will of the Father which hath sent me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Οὐ δύναμαι ἐγὼ ποιεῖν ἀπ᾽ ἐμαυτοῦ οὐδέν· καθὼς ἀκούω κρίνω, καὶ ἡ κρίσις ἡ ἐμὴ δικαία ἐστίν, ὅτι οὐ ζητῶ τὸ θέλημα τὸ ἐμὸν ἀλλὰ τὸ θέλημα τοῦ πέμψαντός με.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]

					% \item[NTXXX*]

					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:31} % *JOHN-5-31 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-31}

			If I bear witness of myself, my witness is not true.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἐὰν ἐγὼ μαρτυρῶ περὶ ἐμαυτοῦ, ἡ μαρτυρία μου οὐκ ἔστιν ἀληθής·}

		% ---

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἄλλος ἐστὶν ὁ μαρτυρῶν περὶ ἐμοῦ, καὶ οἶδα ὅτι ἀληθής ἐστιν ἡ μαρτυρία ἣν μαρτυρεῖ περὶ ἐμοῦ.}

		% ---

			But I receive not testimony from man: but these things I say, that ye might be saved.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐγὼ δὲ οὐ παρὰ ἀνθρώπου τὴν μαρτυρίαν λαμβάνω, ἀλλὰ ταῦτα λέγω ἵνα ὑμεῖς σωθῆτε.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-35}

			He was a burning and a shining light: and ye were willing for a season to rejoice in his light.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐκεῖνος ἦν ὁ λύχνος ὁ καιόμενος καὶ φαίνων, ὑμεῖς δὲ ἠθελήσατε ἀγαλλιαθῆναι πρὸς ὥραν ἐν τῷ φωτὶ αὐτοῦ·}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:36} % *JOHN-5-36 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-36}

			But I have greater witness than that of John: for the works which the Father hath given me to finish, the same works that I do, bear witness of me, that the Father hath sent me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐγὼ δὲ ἔχω τὴν μαρτυρίαν μείζω τοῦ Ἰωάννου, τὰ γὰρ ἔργα ἃ δέδωκέν μοι ὁ πατὴρ ἵνα} \hyperref[TELEIOO]{\grl{τελειώσω}} \gr{αὐτά, αὐτὰ τὰ ἔργα ἃ ποιῶ, μαρτυρεῖ περὶ ἐμοῦ ὅτι ὁ πατήρ με ἀπέσταλκεν,}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-37}

			And the Father himself, which hath sent me, hath borne witness of me. Ye have neither heard his voice at any time, nor seen his shape.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ ὁ πέμψας με πατὴρ ἐκεῖνος μεμαρτύρηκεν περὶ ἐμοῦ. οὔτε φωνὴν αὐτοῦ πώποτε ἀκηκόατε οὔτε εἶδος αὐτοῦ ἑωράκατε,}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ τὸν λόγον αὐτοῦ οὐκ ἔχετε ἐν ὑμῖν μένοντα, ὅτι ὃν ἀπέστειλεν ἐκεῖνος τούτῳ ὑμεῖς οὐ πιστεύετε.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:41} % *JOHN-5-41 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-41}

			I receive not honour%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Note that this is \gr{δόξα} and not \gr{τιμή}. SBLG at least has no apparatus here and so I don't think there is any tradition of having \gr{τιμή} instead. The reason I'm bringing it up is that I'm wondering about the connection to the priesthood and \hyperref[HEBREWS-5-4]{Hebrews 5:4}. See verse 44, same thing, no apparatus.
					}
			from men.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δόξαν παρὰ ἀνθρώπων οὐ λαμβάνω,}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀλλὰ ἔγνωκα ὑμᾶς ὅτι τὴν ἀγάπην τοῦ θεοῦ οὐκ ἔχετε ἐν ἑαυτοῖς.}

		\paragraph{John 5:43} % *JOHN-5-43 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-43}

			I am come in my Father's name, and ye receive me not: if another shall come in his own name, him ye will receive.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐγὼ ἐλήλυθα ἐν τῷ ὀνόματι τοῦ πατρός μου καὶ οὐ λαμβάνετέ με· ἐὰν ἄλλος ἔλθῃ ἐν τῷ ὀνόματι τῷ ἰδίῳ, ἐκεῖνον λήμψεσθε.}

		\paragraph{John 5:44} % *JOHN-5-44 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-44}

			How can ye believe, which receive honour%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					See notes at verse 41 above.
					}
			one of another, and seek not the honour that cometh from God only?

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{πῶς δύνασθε ὑμεῖς πιστεῦσαι, δόξαν παρ᾽ ἀλλήλων λαμβάνοντες, καὶ τὴν δόξαν τὴν παρὰ τοῦ μόνου θεοῦ οὐ ζητεῖτε;}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 5:45} % *JOHN-5-45 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-5-45}

			Do not think that I will accuse you to the Father: there is one that accuseth you, even Moses, in whom ye
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					\gr{ἐλπίζω}; a more pedantic translation could be, ``in whom you place your confidence (as a basis for your hope).'' The base meaning of \gr{ἐλπίζω} involves looking forward to something or hoping for something. See \hyperref[ETHER-12-4]{Ether 12:4} and notes there.
					}%
			trust.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{μὴ δοκεῖτε ὅτι ἐγὼ κατηγορήσω ὑμῶν πρὸς τὸν πατέρα· ἔστιν ὁ κατηγορῶν ὑμῶν Μωϋσῆς, εἰς ὃν ὑμεῖς ἠλπίκατε.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]

					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---

			For had ye believed Moses, ye would have believed me: for he wrote of me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ γὰρ ἐπιστεύετε Μωϋσεῖ, ἐπιστεύετε ἂν ἐμοί, περὶ γὰρ ἐμοῦ ἐκεῖνος ἔγραψεν.}

		% ---

			But if ye believe not his writings, how shall ye believe my words?

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ δὲ τοῖς ἐκείνου γράμμασιν οὐ πιστεύετε, πῶς τοῖς ἐμοῖς ῥήμασιν πιστεύσετε;}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

	\subsubsection{John 6} % *JOHN-6

		\label{JOHN-6}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 6:1} % *JOHN-6-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-6-1}

			AFTER these things Jesus went over the sea of Galilee, which is the sea of Tiberias.

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Μετὰ ταῦτα ἀπῆλθεν ὁ Ἰησοῦς πέραν τῆς θαλάσσης τῆς Γαλιλαίας τῆς Τιβεριάδος.}

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἠκολούθει δὲ αὐτῷ ὄχλος πολύς, ὅτι ἐθεώρουν τὰ σημεῖα ἃ ἐποίει ἐπὶ τῶν ἀσθενούντων.}

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀνῆλθεν δὲ εἰς τὸ ὄρος Ἰησοῦς, καὶ ἐκεῖ ἐκάθητο μετὰ τῶν μαθητῶν αὐτοῦ.}

			And the passover, a feast of the Jews, was nigh.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἦν δὲ ἐγγὺς τὸ πάσχα, ἡ ἑορτὴ τῶν Ἰουδαίων.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-6-5}

			When Jesus then lifted up his eyes, and saw a great company come unto him, he saith unto Philip, Whence shall we buy bread, that these may eat?	

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐπάρας οὖν τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς ὁ Ἰησοῦς καὶ θεασάμενος ὅτι πολὺς ὄχλος ἔρχεται πρὸς αὐτὸν λέγει πρὸς Φίλιππον· Πόθεν ἀγοράσωμεν ἄρτους ἵνα φάγωσιν οὗτοι;}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 6:6} % *JOHN-6-6 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-6-6}

			And this he said to prove him: for he himself knew what he would do.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τοῦτο δὲ ἔλεγεν πειράζων αὐτόν, αὐτὸς γὰρ ᾔδει τί ἔμελλεν ποιεῖν.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 6:45} % *JOHN-6-45 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-6-45}

			It is written in the prophets,%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					ZIBBC NT 02 146: ``The expression `the Prophets' may refer to the general tenor of Old Testament prophetic teaching or to `the Prophets' as a division of the Old Testament. `They will all be taught by God' paraphrases \hyperref[ISAIAH-54-13]{Isaiah 54:13} (cf. \hyperref[JEREMIAH-31-34]{Jer. 31:34}). In Judaism, to learn the Torah was to be taught by God himself. The expression `disciples of God' (i.e., the ones taught by God) is also found in the Qumran writings (CD 20:4; 1QH 10:39; 15:10, 14). See also the messianic parallel in \textit{Psalms of Solomon} 17:32: `And he will be a righteous king over them, taught by God...and their king shall be the Lord Messiah' (prior to A.D. 70).''
					}
			And they shall be all taught of God. Every man therefore that hath heard, and hath learned of the Father, cometh unto me.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔστιν γεγραμμένον ἐν τοῖς προφήταις· Καὶ ἔσονται πάντες διδακτοὶ θεοῦ· πᾶς ὁ ἀκούσας παρὰ τοῦ πατρὸς καὶ μαθὼν ἔρχεται πρὸς ἐμέ.}

		\paragraph{John 6:51} % *JOHN-6-51 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-6-51}

			I am the living bread which came down from heaven: if any man eat of this bread, he shall live for ever: and the bread that I will give is my flesh, which I will give for the life of the world.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ ἄρτος ὁ ζῶν ὁ ἐκ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ καταβάς· ἐάν τις φάγῃ ἐκ τούτου τοῦ ἄρτου ζήσει εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα, καὶ ὁ ἄρτος δὲ ὃν ἐγὼ δώσω ἡ σάρξ μού ἐστιν ὑπὲρ τῆς τοῦ κόσμου ζωῆς.}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπεν οὖν αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν, ἐὰν μὴ φάγητε τὴν σάρκα τοῦ υἱοῦ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου καὶ πίητε αὐτοῦ τὸ αἷμα, οὐκ ἔχετε ζωὴν ἐν ἑαυτοῖς.}

	\subsubsection{John 7} % *JOHN-7 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-7}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 7:1} % *JOHN-7-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-7-1}

			AFTER these things Jesus walked in Galilee: for he would not walk in Jewry, because the Jews sought to kill him.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Καὶ μετὰ ταῦτα περιεπάτει ὁ Ἰησοῦς ἐν τῇ Γαλιλαίᾳ, οὐ γὰρ ἤθελεν ἐν τῇ Ἰουδαίᾳ περιπατεῖν, ὅτι ἐζήτουν αὐτὸν οἱ Ἰουδαῖοι ἀποκτεῖναι.}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]

			Moses therefore gave unto you circumcision; (not because it is of Moses, but of the fathers;) and ye on the sabbath day circumcise a man.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{διὰ τοῦτο Μωϋσῆς δέδωκεν ὑμῖν τὴν περιτομήν— οὐχ ὅτι ἐκ τοῦ Μωϋσέως ἐστὶν ἀλλ᾽ ἐκ τῶν πατέρων— καὶ ἐν σαββάτῳ περιτέμνετε ἄνθρωπον.}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἰ περιτομὴν λαμβάνει ἄνθρωπος ἐν σαββάτῳ ἵνα μὴ λυθῇ ὁ νόμος Μωϋσέως, ἐμοὶ χολᾶτε ὅτι ὅλον ἄνθρωπον ὑγιῆ ἐποίησα ἐν σαββάτῳ;}

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπον οὖν αὐτῷ οἱ Φαρισαῖοι· Σὺ περὶ σεαυτοῦ μαρτυρεῖς· ἡ μαρτυρία σου οὐκ ἔστιν ἀληθής.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 8:14} % *JOHN-8-14 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-8-14}

			Jesus answered and said unto them, Though I bear record of myself, yet my record is true: for I know whence I came, and whither I go; but ye cannot tell whence I come, and whither I go.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀπεκρίθη Ἰησοῦς καὶ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Κἂν ἐγὼ μαρτυρῶ περὶ ἐμαυτοῦ, ἀληθής ἐστιν ἡ μαρτυρία μου, ὅτι οἶδα πόθεν ἦλθον καὶ ποῦ ὑπάγω· ὑμεῖς δὲ οὐκ οἴδατε πόθεν ἔρχομαι ἢ ποῦ ὑπάγω.}

		\paragraph{John 8:16} % *JOHN-8-16 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-8-16}

			And yet if I judge, my judgment is true: for I am not alone, but I and the Father that sent me.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{καὶ ἐὰν κρίνω δὲ ἐγώ, ἡ κρίσις ἡ ἐμὴ ἀληθινή ἐστιν, ὅτι μόνος οὐκ εἰμί, ἀλλ᾽ ἐγὼ καὶ ὁ πέμψας με πατήρ.}

			They answered and said unto him, Abraham is our father. Jesus saith unto them, If ye were Abraham's children, ye would do the works of Abraham.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀπεκρίθησαν καὶ εἶπαν αὐτῷ· Ὁ πατὴρ ἡμῶν Ἀβραάμ ἐστιν. λέγει αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Εἰ τέκνα τοῦ Ἀβραάμ ἐστε, τὰ ἔργα τοῦ Ἀβραὰμ ἐποιεῖτε·}

		\paragraph{John 8:40} % *JOHN-8-40 
		% ---

			But now ye seek to kill me, a man that hath told you the truth, which I have heard of God: this did not Abraham.

				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{νῦν δὲ ζητεῖτέ με ἀποκτεῖναι, ἄνθρωπον ὃς τὴν ἀλήθειαν ὑμῖν λελάληκα ἣν ἤκουσα παρὰ τοῦ θεοῦ· τοῦτο Ἀβραὰμ οὐκ ἐποίησεν.}

		% ---

			Ye do the deeds of your father. Then said they to him, We be not born of fornication; we have one Father, even God.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὑμεῖς ποιεῖτε τὰ ἔργα τοῦ πατρὸς ὑμῶν. εἶπαν αὐτῷ· Ἡμεῖς ἐκ πορνείας οὐ γεγεννήμεθα· ἕνα πατέρα ἔχομεν τὸν θεόν.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-8-42}

			Jesus said unto them, If God were your Father, ye would love me: for I proceeded forth and came from God; neither came I of myself, but he sent me.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπεν αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Εἰ ὁ θεὸς πατὴρ ὑμῶν ἦν ἠγαπᾶτε ἂν ἐμέ, ἐγὼ γὰρ ἐκ τοῦ θεοῦ ἐξῆλθον καὶ ἥκω· οὐδὲ γὰρ ἀπ᾽ ἐμαυτοῦ ἐλήλυθα, ἀλλ᾽ ἐκεῖνός με ἀπέστειλεν.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 8:44} % *JOHN-8-44 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-8-44}

			Ye are of your father the devil, and the lusts of your father ye will do. He was a murderer from the beginning, and abode not in the truth, because there is no truth in him. When he speaketh a lie, he speaketh of his own: for he is a liar, and the father of it.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὑμεῖς ἐκ τοῦ πατρὸς τοῦ διαβόλου ἐστὲ καὶ τὰς ἐπιθυμίας τοῦ πατρὸς ὑμῶν θέλετε ποιεῖν. ἐκεῖνος ἀνθρωποκτόνος ἦν ἀπ᾽ ἀρχῆς, καὶ ἐν τῇ ἀληθείᾳ οὐκ ἔστηκεν, ὅτι οὐκ ἔστιν ἀλήθεια ἐν αὐτῷ. ὅταν λαλῇ τὸ ψεῦδος, ἐκ τῶν ἰδίων λαλεῖ, ὅτι ψεύστης ἐστὶν καὶ ὁ πατὴρ αὐτοῦ.}

			Then said the Jews unto him, Now we know that thou hast a devil. Abraham is dead, and the prophets; and thou sayest, If a man keep my saying, he shall never taste of death.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπον αὐτῷ οἱ Ἰουδαῖοι· Νῦν ἐγνώκαμεν ὅτι δαιμόνιον ἔχεις. Ἀβραὰμ ἀπέθανεν καὶ οἱ προφῆται, καὶ σὺ λέγεις· Ἐάν τις τὸν λόγον μου τηρήσῃ, οὐ μὴ γεύσηται θανάτου εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα·}

		% ---

		% ---

			Jesus answered, If I honour%

	\subsubsection{John 9} % *JOHN-9 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-9}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 9:1} % *JOHN-9-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-9-1}

			AND as Jesus passed by, he saw a man which was blind from his birth.

			\begin{framed}
			\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}

			Therefore said some of the Pharisees, This man is not of God, because he keepeth not the sabbath day. Others said, How can a man that is a sinner do such miracles? And there was a division among them.

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔλεγον οὖν ἐκ τῶν Φαρισαίων τινές· Οὐκ ἔστιν οὗτος παρὰ θεοῦ ὁ ἄνθρωπος, ὅτι τὸ σάββατον οὐ τηρεῖ. ἄλλοι ἔλεγον· Πῶς δύναται ἄνθρωπος ἁμαρτωλὸς τοιαῦτα σημεῖα ποιεῖν; καὶ σχίσμα ἦν ἐν αὐτοῖς.}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{πῶς δὲ νῦν βλέπει οὐκ οἴδαμεν, ἢ τίς ἤνοιξεν αὐτοῦ τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς ἡμεῖς οὐκ οἴδαμεν· αὐτὸν ἐρωτήσατε, ἡλικίαν ἔχει, αὐτὸς περὶ ἑαυτοῦ λαλήσει.}

	\subsubsection{John 10} % *JOHN-10 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-10}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 10:1} % *JOHN-10-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-10-1}
			
			VERILY, verily, I say unto you, He that entereth not by the door into the sheepfold, but climbeth up some other way, the same is a thief and a robber.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν, ὁ μὴ εἰσερχόμενος διὰ τῆς θύρας εἰς τὴν αὐλὴν τῶν προβάτων ἀλλὰ ἀναβαίνων ἀλλαχόθεν ἐκεῖνος κλέπτης ἐστὶν καὶ λῃστής·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]

					% \item[NTXX*]
					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 10:2} % *JOHN-10-2 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-10-2}

			But he that entereth in by the door is the shepherd of the sheep.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὁ δὲ εἰσερχόμενος διὰ τῆς θύρας ποιμήν ἐστιν τῶν προβάτων.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]

					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 10:3} % *JOHN-10-3 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-10-3}

			To him the porter openeth; and the sheep hear his voice: and he calleth his own sheep by name, and leadeth them out.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τούτῳ ὁ θυρωρὸς ἀνοίγει, καὶ τὰ πρόβατα τῆς φωνῆς αὐτοῦ ἀκούει καὶ τὰ ἴδια πρόβατα φωνεῖ κατ᾽ ὄνομα καὶ ἐξάγει αὐτά.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]

					% \item[NTXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXX*]
					% \item[NTXXXXXX*]
				\end{compactdesc}
			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 10:4} % *JOHN-10-4 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-10-4}

			And when he putteth forth his own sheep, he goeth before them, and the sheep follow him: for they know his voice.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὅταν τὰ ἴδια πάντα ἐκβάλῃ, ἔμπροσθεν αὐτῶν πορεύεται, καὶ τὰ πρόβατα αὐτῷ ἀκολουθεῖ, ὅτι οἴδασιν τὴν φωνὴν αὐτοῦ·}
					% \item[SNT]
					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]

					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἦν δέ τις ἀσθενῶν, Λάζαρος ἀπὸ Βηθανίας ἐκ τῆς κώμης Μαρίας καὶ Μάρθας τῆς ἀδελφῆς αὐτῆς.}

			His disciples say unto him, Master, the Jews of late sought to stone thee; and goest thou thither again?

			\begin{framed}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἡ οὖν Μαριὰμ ὡς ἦλθεν ὅπου ἦν Ἰησοῦς ἰδοῦσα αὐτὸν ἔπεσεν αὐτοῦ πρὸς τοὺς πόδας, λέγουσα αὐτῷ· Κύριε, εἰ ἦς ὧδε οὐκ ἄν μου ἀπέθανεν ὁ ἀδελφός.}

			\label{JOHN-11-53}

			Then from that day forth they took counsel together for to put him to death.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀπ᾽ ἐκείνης οὖν τῆς ἡμέρας ἐβουλεύσαντο ἵνα ἀποκτείνωσιν αὐτόν.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 11:54} % *JOHN-11-54 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-11-54}

			Jesus therefore walked no more openly among the Jews; but went thence unto a country near to the wilderness, into a city called Ephraim, and there continued with his disciples.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ὁ οὖν Ἰησοῦς οὐκέτι παρρησίᾳ περιεπάτει ἐν τοῖς Ἰουδαίοις, ἀλλὰ ἀπῆλθεν ἐκεῖθεν εἰς τὴν χώραν ἐγγὺς τῆς ἐρήμου, εἰς Ἐφραὶμ λεγομένην πόλιν, κἀκεῖ ἔμεινεν μετὰ τῶν μαθητῶν.}

			\end{framed}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 11:55} % *JOHN-11-55 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-11-55}

			And the Jews' passover was nigh at hand: and many went out of the country up to Jerusalem before the passover, to purify themselves.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἦν δὲ ἐγγὺς τὸ πάσχα τῶν Ἰουδαίων, καὶ ἀνέβησαν πολλοὶ εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα ἐκ τῆς χώρας πρὸ τοῦ πάσχα ἵνα ἁγνίσωσιν ἑαυτούς.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 11:56} % *JOHN-11-56 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-11-56}

			Then sought they for Jesus, and spake among themselves, as they stood in the temple, What think ye, that he will not come to the feast?

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐζήτουν οὖν τὸν Ἰησοῦν καὶ ἔλεγον μετ᾽ ἀλλήλων ἐν τῷ ἱερῷ ἑστηκότες· Τί δοκεῖ ὑμῖν; ὅτι οὐ μὴ ἔλθῃ εἰς τὴν ἑορτήν;}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-11-57}

			Now both the chief priests and the Pharisees had given a commandment, that, if any man knew where he were, he should shew it, that they might take him.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{δεδώκεισαν δὲ οἱ ἀρχιερεῖς καὶ οἱ Φαρισαῖοι ἐντολὰς ἵνα ἐάν τις γνῷ ποῦ ἐστιν μηνύσῃ, ὅπως πιάσωσιν αὐτόν.}

	\subsubsection{John 12} % *JOHN-12 
	% -----
		\label{JOHN-12}
		
		% ---
		\paragraph{John 12:1} % *JOHN-12-1 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-1}

			THEN Jesus six days before the passover came to Bethany, where Lazarus was which had been dead, whom he raised from the dead.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ὁ οὖν Ἰησοῦς πρὸ ἓξ ἡμερῶν τοῦ πάσχα ἦλθεν εἰς Βηθανίαν, ὅπου ἦν Λάζαρος, ὃν ἤγειρεν ἐκ νεκρῶν Ἰησοῦς.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-2}

			There they made him a supper; and Martha served: but Lazarus was one of them that sat at the table with him.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐποίησαν οὖν αὐτῷ δεῖπνον ἐκεῖ, καὶ ἡ Μάρθα διηκόνει, ὁ δὲ Λάζαρος εἷς ἦν ἐκ τῶν ἀνακειμένων σὺν αὐτῷ·}

		\paragraph{John 12:3} % *JOHN-12-3 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-3}

			Then took Mary a pound of ointment of spikenard, very costly, and anointed the feet of Jesus, and wiped his feet with her hair: and the house was filled with the odour of the ointment.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἡ οὖν Μαριὰμ λαβοῦσα λίτραν μύρου νάρδου πιστικῆς πολυτίμου ἤλειψεν τοὺς πόδας τοῦ Ἰησοῦ καὶ ἐξέμαξεν ταῖς θριξὶν αὐτῆς τοὺς πόδας αὐτοῦ· ἡ δὲ οἰκία ἐπληρώθη ἐκ τῆς ὀσμῆς τοῦ μύρου.}

		% ---

			Then saith one of his disciples, Judas Iscariot, Simon's son, which should betray him,

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει δὲ Ἰούδας ὁ Ἰσκαριώτης εἷς τῶν μαθητῶν αὐτοῦ, ὁ μέλλων αὐτὸν παραδιδόναι·}

		% ---

			Why was not this ointment sold for three hundred pence, and given to the poor?

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Διὰ τί τοῦτο τὸ μύρον οὐκ ἐπράθη τριακοσίων δηναρίων καὶ ἐδόθη πτωχοῖς;}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-6}

			This he said, not that he cared for the poor; but because he was a thief, and had the bag, and bare what was put therein.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπεν δὲ τοῦτο οὐχ ὅτι περὶ τῶν πτωχῶν ἔμελεν αὐτῷ, ἀλλ᾽ ὅτι κλέπτης ἦν καὶ τὸ γλωσσόκομον ἔχων τὰ βαλλόμενα ἐβάσταζεν.}

		% ---

			Then said Jesus, Let her alone: against the day of my burying hath she kept this.

				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπεν οὖν ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Ἄφες αὐτήν, ἵνα εἰς τὴν ἡμέραν τοῦ ἐνταφιασμοῦ μου τηρήσῃ αὐτό·}

		% ---

			For the poor always ye have with you; but me ye have not always.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{τοὺς πτωχοὺς γὰρ πάντοτε ἔχετε μεθ᾽ ἑαυτῶν, ἐμὲ δὲ οὐ πάντοτε ἔχετε.}

		\paragraph{John 12:9} % *JOHN-12-9 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-9}

			Much people of the Jews therefore knew that he was there: and they came not for Jesus' sake only, but that they might see Lazarus also, whom he had raised from the dead.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Ἔγνω οὖν ὄχλος πολὺς ἐκ τῶν Ἰουδαίων ὅτι ἐκεῖ ἐστιν, καὶ ἦλθον οὐ διὰ τὸν Ἰησοῦν μόνον, ἀλλ᾽ ἵνα καὶ τὸν Λάζαρον ἴδωσιν ὃν ἤγειρεν ἐκ νεκρῶν.}

			But the chief priests consulted that they might put Lazarus also to death;

				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐβουλεύσαντο δὲ οἱ ἀρχιερεῖς ἵνα καὶ τὸν Λάζαρον ἀποκτείνωσιν,}

		% ---

			Because that by reason of him many of the Jews went away, and believed on Jesus.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ὅτι πολλοὶ δι᾽ αὐτὸν ὑπῆγον τῶν Ἰουδαίων καὶ ἐπίστευον εἰς τὸν Ἰησοῦν.}

		\paragraph{John 12:12} % *JOHN-12-12 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-12}

			On the next day much people that were come to the feast, when they heard that Jesus was coming to Jerusalem,

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Τῇ ἐπαύριον ὁ ὄχλος πολὺς ὁ ἐλθὼν εἰς τὴν ἑορτήν, ἀκούσαντες ὅτι ἔρχεται ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα,}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 12:13} % *JOHN-12-13 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-13}

			Took branches of palm trees, and went forth to meet him, and cried, Hosanna:%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					For ``Hosanna,'' see note at \hyperref[MARK-11-9]{Mark 11:9}.
					}
			Blessed is the King of Israel that cometh in the name of the Lord.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔλαβον τὰ βαΐα τῶν φοινίκων καὶ ἐξῆλθον εἰς ὑπάντησιν αὐτῷ, καὶ ἐκραύγαζον· Ὡσαννά, εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι κυρίου, καὶ ὁ βασιλεὺς τοῦ Ἰσραήλ.}

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-14}

			And Jesus, when he had found a young ass, sat thereon; as it is written,

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{εὑρὼν δὲ ὁ Ἰησοῦς ὀνάριον ἐκάθισεν ἐπ᾽ αὐτό, καθώς ἐστιν γεγραμμένον·}

		\paragraph{John 12:15} % *JOHN-12-15 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-15}

			Fear not, daughter of Sion: behold, thy King cometh, sitting on an ass's colt.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Μὴ φοβοῦ, θυγάτηρ Σιών· ἰδοὺ ὁ βασιλεύς σου ἔρχεται, καθήμενος ἐπὶ πῶλον ὄνου.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 12:16} % *JOHN-12-16 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-16}

			These things understood not his disciples at the first: but when Jesus was glorified, then remembered they that these things were written of him, and that they had done these things unto him.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ταῦτα οὐκ ἔγνωσαν αὐτοῦ οἱ μαθηταὶ τὸ πρῶτον, ἀλλ᾽ ὅτε ἐδοξάσθη Ἰησοῦς τότε ἐμνήσθησαν ὅτι ταῦτα ἦν ἐπ᾽ αὐτῷ γεγραμμένα καὶ ταῦτα ἐποίησαν αὐτῷ.}

		\paragraph{John 12:17} % *JOHN-12-17 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-12-17}

			The people therefore that was with him when he called Lazarus out of his grave, and raised him from the dead, bare record.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐμαρτύρει οὖν ὁ ὄχλος ὁ ὢν μετ᾽ αὐτοῦ ὅτε τὸν Λάζαρον ἐφώνησεν ἐκ τοῦ μνημείου καὶ ἤγειρεν αὐτὸν ἐκ νεκρῶν.}

					% \item[BSV]
					% \item[ASV]
					% \item[AB ]
					% \item[SPNT]
					% \item[NTR1]
					% \item[NTR2]
					% \item[NTX*]
					% \item[NTXX*]

		% ---

			Then Jesus said unto them, Yet a little while is the light with you. Walk while ye have the light, lest darkness come upon you: for he that walketh in darkness knoweth not whither he goeth.

					\item[SBLG] \gr{εἶπεν οὖν αὐτοῖς ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Ἔτι μικρὸν χρόνον τὸ φῶς ἐν ὑμῖν ἐστιν. περιπατεῖτε ὡς τὸ φῶς ἔχετε, ἵνα μὴ σκοτία ὑμᾶς καταλάβῃ, καὶ ὁ περιπατῶν ἐν τῇ σκοτίᾳ οὐκ οἶδεν ποῦ ὑπάγει.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:1} % *JOHN-13-1 
		% ---

			NOW before the feast of the passover, when Jesus knew that his hour was come that he should depart out of this world unto the Father, having loved his own which were in the world, he loved them unto the end.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Πρὸ δὲ τῆς ἑορτῆς τοῦ πάσχα εἰδὼς ὁ Ἰησοῦς ὅτι ἦλθεν αὐτοῦ ἡ ὥρα ἵνα μεταβῇ ἐκ τοῦ κόσμου τούτου πρὸς τὸν πατέρα ἀγαπήσας τοὺς ἰδίους τοὺς ἐν τῷ κόσμῳ εἰς τέλος ἠγάπησεν αὐτούς.}

		% ---

			Then cometh he to Simon Peter: and Peter saith unto him, Lord, dost thou wash my feet?

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἔρχεται οὖν πρὸς Σίμωνα Πέτρον. λέγει αὐτῷ· Κύριε, σύ μου νίπτεις τοὺς πόδας;}

		% ---

			Jesus answered and said unto him, What I do thou knowest not now; but thou shalt know hereafter.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀπεκρίθη Ἰησοῦς καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ὃ ἐγὼ ποιῶ σὺ οὐκ οἶδας ἄρτι, γνώσῃ δὲ μετὰ ταῦτα.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:8} % *JOHN-13-8 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-8}

			Peter saith unto him, Thou shalt never wash my feet. Jesus answered him, If I wash thee not, thou hast no part with me.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει αὐτῷ Πέτρος· Οὐ μὴ νίψῃς μου τοὺς πόδας εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα. ἀπεκρίθη Ἰησοῦς αὐτῷ· Ἐὰν μὴ νίψω σε, οὐκ ἔχεις μέρος μετ᾽ ἐμοῦ.}

			Simon Peter saith unto him, Lord, not my feet only, but also my hands and my head.

		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-10}

			Jesus saith to him, He that is washed needeth not save to wash his feet, but is clean every whit: and ye are clean, but not all.

				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Ὁ λελουμένος οὐκ ἔχει χρείαν εἰ μὴ τοὺς πόδας νίψασθαι, ἀλλ᾽ ἔστιν καθαρὸς ὅλος· καὶ ὑμεῖς καθαροί ἐστε, ἀλλ᾽ οὐχὶ πάντες.}

					\item[SBLG] \gr{ᾔδει γὰρ τὸν παραδιδόντα αὐτόν· διὰ τοῦτο εἶπεν ὅτι Οὐχὶ πάντες καθαροί ἐστε.}

			I speak not of you all: I know whom I have chosen: but that the scripture may be fulfilled, He that eateth bread with me hath lifted up his heel%
				\footnote{%
					% \label{}%
					Reminds me of \hyperref[GENESIS-3-15]{Genesis 3:15}. It also reminds me of the endowment ceremony, where Elohim tells Satan, talking about the descendants of Eve, ``Thou mayest have power to bruise his heel, but he shall have power to crush thy head.''
					}
			against me.

				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{οὐ περὶ πάντων ὑμῶν λέγω· ἐγὼ οἶδα τίνας ἐξελεξάμην· ἀλλ᾽ ἵνα ἡ γραφὴ πληρωθῇ· Ὁ τρώγων μου τὸν ἄρτον ἐπῆρεν ἐπ᾽ ἐμὲ τὴν πτέρναν αὐτοῦ.}

			Jesus answered, He it is, to whom I shall give a sop,%

					\gr{ψωμίον}, BDAG 981a, ``\textbf{(small) piece, bit of bread}.''}
			when I have dipped it. And when he had dipped the sop, he gave it to Judas Iscariot, the son of Simon.

				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀποκρίνεται ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Ἐκεῖνός ἐστιν ᾧ ἐγὼ βάψω τὸ ψωμίον καὶ δώσω αὐτῷ· βάψας οὖν τὸ ψωμίον δίδωσιν Ἰούδᾳ Σίμωνος Ἰσκαριώτου.}

		\paragraph{John 13:34} % *JOHN-13-34 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-34}

			A new commandment I give unto you, That ye love one another; as I have loved you, that ye also love one another.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐντολὴν καινὴν δίδωμι ὑμῖν ἵνα ἀγαπᾶτε ἀλλήλους, καθὼς ἠγάπησα ὑμᾶς ἵνα καὶ ὑμεῖς ἀγαπᾶτε ἀλλήλους.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:35} % *JOHN-13-35 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-35}

			By this shall all men know that ye are my disciples, if ye have love one to another.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἐν τούτῳ γνώσονται πάντες ὅτι ἐμοὶ μαθηταί ἐστε, ἐὰν ἀγάπην ἔχητε ἐν ἀλλήλοις.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:36} % *JOHN-13-36 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-36}

			Simon Peter said unto him, Lord, whither goest thou? Jesus answered him, Whither I go, thou canst not follow me now; but thou shalt follow me afterwards.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{Λέγει αὐτῷ Σίμων Πέτρος· Κύριε, ποῦ ὑπάγεις; ἀπεκρίθη Ἰησοῦς· Ὅπου ὑπάγω οὐ δύνασαί μοι νῦν ἀκολουθῆσαι, ἀκολουθήσεις δὲ ὕστερον.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:37} % *JOHN-13-37 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-37}

			Peter said unto him, Lord, why cannot I follow thee now? I will lay down my life for thy sake.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ Πέτρος· Κύριε, διὰ τί οὐ δύναμαί σοι ἀκολουθῆσαι ἄρτι; τὴν ψυχήν μου ὑπὲρ σοῦ θήσω.}

		% ---
		\paragraph{John 13:38} % *JOHN-13-38 
		% ---
			\label{JOHN-13-38}

			Jesus answered him, Wilt thou lay down thy life for my sake? Verily, verily, I say unto thee, The cock shall not crow, till thou hast denied me thrice.

			\begin{framed}
				\scriptsize
				\begin{compactdesc}
					\item[SBLG] \gr{ἀποκρίνεται Ἰησοῦς· Τὴν ψυχήν σου ὑπὲρ ἐμοῦ θήσεις; ἀμὴν ἀμὴν λέγω σοι, οὐ μὴ ἀλέκτωρ φωνήσῃ ἕως οὗ ἀρνήσῃ με τρίς.}
